2026-08-23T10:17:24.7478914Z    just set the minSdkVersion in your build.gradle or AndroidManifest.xml
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7479641Z    files.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7479862Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7480250Z    If your code is deliberately accessing newer APIs, and you have ensured
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7481195Z    (e.g. with conditional execution) that this code will only ever be called
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7482135Z    on a supported platform, then you can annotate your class or method with
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7483086Z    the @TargetApi annotation specifying the local minimum SDK to apply, such
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7484049Z    as @TargetApi(11), such that this check considers 11 rather than your
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7484876Z    manifest file's minimum SDK as the required API level.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7485352Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7485765Z    If you are deliberately setting android: attributes in style definitions,
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7486991Z    make sure you place this in a values-vNN folder in order to avoid running
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7487996Z    into runtime conflicts on certain devices where manufacturers have added
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7489004Z    custom attributes whose ids conflict with the new ones on later platforms.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7489581Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7490021Z    Similarly, you can use tools:targetApi="11" in an XML file to indicate that
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7490961Z    the element will only be inflated in an adequate context.

2026-08-23T10:17:24.7498919Z    recent than your targetSdkVersion specifies that it has been tested with,
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7499938Z    various compatibility modes kick in. This ensures that your application
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7500905Z    continues to work, but it may look out of place. For example, if the
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7501885Z    targetSdkVersion is less than 14, your app may get an option button in the
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7502637Z    UI.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7502870Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7503286Z    To fix this issue, set the targetSdkVersion to the highest available value.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7504267Z    Then test your app to make sure everything works correctly. You may want to
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7507470Z    consult the compatibility notes to see what changes apply to each version
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7508280Z    you are adding support for:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7509074Z    https://developer.android.com/reference/android/os/Build.VERSION_CODES.html

2026-08-23T10:17:24.7520121Z    Explanation for issues of type "SuspiciousIndentation":
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7521001Z    This check looks for cases where the indentation suggests a grouping that
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7521952Z    isn't actually there in the code. A common example of this would be
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7522653Z    something like
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7523077Z    if (column > width)
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7523505Z        line++
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7523920Z        column = 0
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7524564Z    Here, the column = 0 line will be executed every single time, not just if
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7525330Z    the condition is true.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7525629Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7525817Z    Available options:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7526095Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7526556Z    **always-run** (default is false):
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7527223Z    Whether this check should be included while editing.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7527673Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7531267Z    While you're editing, it's common to have a temporary situation where you have suspicious indentation scenarios -- e.g. you start typing an `if` statement on the line above something you want to make conditional, and you haven't indented it yet. It can be distracting and misleading to suddenly have both statements light up as errors. Therefore, lint will avoid including this check when running on the fly in the editor, unless it looks like the file has not been recently edited. With this option, you can turn it on in all cases.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7534560Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7534993Z    To configure this option, use a `lint.xml` file with an <option> like this:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7535604Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7535771Z    ```xml
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7536151Z    <lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7537088Z        <issue id="SuspiciousIndentation">
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7538076Z            <option name="always-run" value="false" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7538682Z        </issue>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7539073Z    </lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7539440Z    ```

2026-08-23T10:17:24.7775626Z    Explanation for issues of type "IntentFilterUniqueDataAttributes":
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7776660Z    <intent-filter> <data> tags should only declare a single unique attribute
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7777471Z    (i.e. scheme OR host, but not both). This better matches the runtime
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7778254Z    behavior of intent filters, as they combine all of the declared data
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7779079Z    attributes into a single matcher which is allowed to handle any combination
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7779780Z    across attribute types.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7780047Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7780404Z    For example, the following two <intent-filter> declarations are the same:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7781055Z    <intent-filter>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7781558Z        <data android:scheme="http" android:host="example.com" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7782266Z        <data android:scheme="https" android:host="example.org" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7782859Z    </intent-filter>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7783098Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7783266Z    <intent-filter>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7783678Z        <data android:scheme="http"/>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7784170Z        <data android:scheme="https"/>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7784693Z        <data android:host="example.com" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7785231Z        <data android:host="example.org" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7785720Z    </intent-filter>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7785949Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7786527Z    They both handle all of the following:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7787083Z    * http://example.com
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7787523Z    * https://example.com
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7787964Z    * http://example.org
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7788381Z    * https://example.org
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7788644Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7789016Z    The second one better communicates the combining behavior and is clearer to
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7789865Z    an external reader that one should not rely on the scheme/host being self
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7790711Z    contained. It is not obvious in the first that http://example.org is also
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7791564Z    matched, which can lead to confusion (or incorrect behavior) with a more
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7792238Z    complex set of schemes/hosts.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7792536Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7792892Z    Note that this does not apply to host + port, as those must be declared in
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7793673Z    the same <data> tag and are only associated with each other.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7794100Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.7794426Z    https://developer.android.com/guide/components/intents-filters

2026-08-23T10:17:24.8181885Z    Explanation for issues of type "StaticFieldLeak":
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182012Z    A static field will leak contexts.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182019Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182253Z    Non-static inner classes have an implicit reference to their outer class.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182462Z    If that outer class is for example a Fragment or Activity, then this
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182682Z    reference means that the long-running handler/loader/task will hold a
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182882Z    reference to the activity which prevents it from getting garbage
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182986Z    collected.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8182995Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8183229Z    Similarly, direct field references to activities and fragments from these
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8183374Z    longer running instances can cause leaks.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8183380Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8183617Z    ViewModel classes should never point to Views or non-application Contexts.

2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235059Z    Explanation for issues of type "UnusedResources":
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235263Z    Unused resources make applications larger and slow down builds.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235269Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235281Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235493Z    The unused resource check can ignore tests. If you want to include
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235734Z    resources that are only referenced from tests, consider packaging them in a
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235853Z    test source set instead.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8235859Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236084Z    You can include test sources in the unused resource check by setting the
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236460Z    system property lint.unused-resources.include-tests =true, and to exclude
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236611Z    them (usually for performance reasons), use
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236764Z    lint.unused-resources.exclude-tests =true.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236868Z    ,
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236880Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236993Z    Available options:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8236999Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8237127Z    **skip-libraries** (default is true):
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8237400Z    Whether the unused resource check should skip reporting unused resources in libraries.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8237406Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8238751Z    Many libraries will declare resources that are part of the library surface; other modules depending on the library will also reference the resources. To avoid reporting all these resources as unused (in the context of a library), the unused resource check normally skips reporting unused resources in libraries. Instead, run the unused resource check on the consuming app module (along with `checkDependencies=true`).
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8238758Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239221Z    However, there are cases where you want to check that all the resources declared in a library are used; in that case, you can disable the skip option.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239354Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239589Z    To configure this option, use a `lint.xml` file with an <option> like this:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239596Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239697Z    ```xml
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239795Z    <lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8239920Z        <issue id="UnusedResources">
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8240077Z            <option name="skip-libraries" value="true" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8240182Z        </issue>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8240279Z    </lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8240373Z    ```

2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525166Z    Explanation for issues of type "UseKtx":
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525395Z    The Android KTX libraries decorates the Android platform SDK as well as
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525630Z    various libraries with more convenient extension functions available from
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525851Z    Kotlin, allowing you to use default parameters, named parameters, and
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525951Z    more.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8525958Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8526076Z    Available options:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8526087Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8526475Z    **remove-defaults** (default is true):
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8526733Z    Whether to skip arguments that match the defaults provided by the extension.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8526739Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527005Z    Extensions often provide default values for some of the parameters. For example:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527115Z    ```kotlin
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527476Z    fun Path.readLines(charset: Charset = Charsets.UTF_8): List<String> { return Files.readAllLines(this, charset) }
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527577Z    ```
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527938Z    This lint check will by default automatically omit parameters that match the default, so if your code was calling
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8527944Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528047Z    ```kotlin
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528184Z    Files.readAllLines(file, Charset.UTF_8)
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528279Z    ```
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528403Z    lint would replace this with
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528504Z    ```kotlin
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528617Z    file.readLines()
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528718Z    ```
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528826Z    rather than
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528831Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8528927Z    ```kotlin
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529055Z    file.readLines(Charset.UTF_8
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529157Z    ```
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529318Z    You can turn this behavior off using this option.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529324Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529564Z    To configure this option, use a `lint.xml` file with an <option> like this:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529570Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529667Z    ```xml
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529764Z    <lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8529879Z        <issue id="UseKtx">
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530046Z            <option name="remove-defaults" value="true" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530152Z        </issue>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530250Z    </lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530350Z    ```
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530356Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530491Z    **require-present** (default is true):
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530657Z    Whether to only offer extensions already available.
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8530667Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531484Z    This option lets you only have lint suggest extension replacements if those extensions are already available on the class path (in other words, you're already depending on the library containing the extension method.)
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531493Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531724Z    To configure this option, use a `lint.xml` file with an <option> like this:
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531730Z 
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531826Z    ```xml
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8531927Z    <lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8532040Z        <issue id="UseKtx">
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8532199Z            <option name="require-present" value="true" />
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8532299Z        </issue>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8532396Z    </lint>
2026-08-23T10:17:24.8532491Z    ```
